8.5.
Proofs of
Theorems

1.2
,
1.7
and
1.1

First, we show that, as a byproduct of the proof of
Theorem

8.1
, we also have a proof of
Theorem

1.2
.

Proof of
Theorem

1.2
.

Apply Steps 1, 4, 6, 7, 10, 11, 12, 14 and 15 in succession.
In general, any reference to absorbing cubes in these steps (see e.g. the end of Step 6) should be skipped as well.
∎

Next, we will show how
Theorem

8.1
can be used to prove
Theorem

1.7
.

Proof of
Theorem

1.7
.

Consider a decomposition of
H
into
k
edge-disjoint subgraphs
H_{1}\cup\dots\cup H_{k}
such that, for every
i\in[k]
, we have
\delta(H_{i})\geq\alpha n/(2k)
.
To see that this is possible, let us randomly partition the edges of
H
so that each
e\in E(H)
is assigned to one of the
H_{i}
’s uniformly at random and independently from all other edges.
Thus, for every
i\in[k]
we have
\mathbb{P}[e\in E(H_{i})]=1/k
.
It follows by
Lemma

4.2
that, for every vertex
x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
and every
i\in[k]
,

\mathbb{P}[d_{H_{i}}(x)\leq\alpha n/(2k)]\leq e^{-\alpha n/(8k)}.

For each
x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
, let
\mathcal{B}(x)
be the event that
d_{H_{i}}(x)\leq\alpha n/(2k)
for some
i\in[k]
.
Hence,
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}(x)]\leq ke^{-\alpha n/(8k)}
for all
x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
.
Observe that
\mathcal{B}(x)
is independent of the collection of events
\{\mathcal{B}(y):{\rm dist}(x,y)\geq 2\}
.
A simple application of
Lemma

4.8
shows that

\mathbb{P}\left[\bigwedge_{x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})}\overline{\mathcal{B}(x)}\right]>0

and, therefore, such a decomposition of
H
exists.

We now consider a similar decomposition of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{\varepsilon}
.
In particular, given
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}^{n}
, we partition its edges into
k
edge-disjoint subgraphs,
Q_{1}\cup\dots\cup Q_{k}
, in such a way that, if
e\in E(\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}^{n})
, then
e
is assigned to one of the
Q_{i}
chosen uniformly at random and independently of all other edges.
Thus, for each
e\in E(\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}^{n})
we have
\mathbb{P}[e\in E(Q_{i})]=1/k
for all
i\in[k]
.
It follows that, for each
i\in[k]
, we have
Q_{i}\sim\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{\varepsilon/k}
.

Let
\Phi
be a constant such that
Theorem

8.1
holds with
\varepsilon/k
,
\alpha/(2k)
and
k+2
playing the roles of
\varepsilon
,
\alpha
and
c
, respectively.
For each
i\in[k]
, apply
Theorem

8.1
with
H_{i}
and
Q_{i}
playing the roles of
H
and
G
, respectively.
We obtain that a.a.s. there exists a subgraph
G_{i}\subseteq Q_{i}
with
\Delta(G_{i})\leq\Phi
such that, for every
F_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
with
\Delta(F_{i})\leq(k+2)\Phi
, the graph
((H_{i}\cup Q_{i})\setminus F_{i}))\cup G_{i}
is Hamiltonian.
Condition on the event that this holds for all
i\in[k]
simultaneously (which holds a.a.s. by a union bound).
Note that, since the graphs
Q_{i}
are pairwise edge-disjoint, so are the different graphs
G_{i}
.

We are now going to find
k
edge-disjoint Hamilton cycles
C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}
iteratively.
For each
i\in[k]
, we proceed as follows.
Let
F_{i}\coloneqq\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}G_{j}\cup\bigcup_{j=1}^{i-1}C_{j}
.
It is clear by construction that
\Delta(F_{i})\leq k(\Phi+2)\leq(k+2)\Phi
.
By the conditioning above, there must be a Hamilton cycle
C_{i}\subseteq((H_{i}\cup Q_{i})\setminus F_{i}))\cup G_{i}
.
Take any such
C_{i}
and proceed.

It remains to prove that
C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}
are pairwise edge-disjoint.
In order to see this, suppose that there exist
i,j\in[k]
with
i<j
such that
E(C_{i})\cap E(C_{j})\neq\varnothing
, and let
e\in E(C_{i})\cap E(C_{j})
.
In order to have
e\in E(C_{i})
, since
G_{j}\subseteq F_{i}\setminus G_{i}
, we must have
e\notin E(G_{j})
.
However, since
e\in F_{j}
by definition, we must have
e\in E(G_{j})
, a contradiction.
∎

Now,
Theorem

1.1
follows as an immediate corollary.

Proof of
Theorem

1.1
.

It is well known (see e.g.
[
12
]
) and easy to show that
\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{1/2-\varepsilon}
a.a.s. contains isolated vertices.
So it suffices to consider
\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{1/2+\varepsilon}
for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
and show that a.a.s. it contains
k
edge-disjoint Hamilton cycles.
Let
0<\delta\ll\varepsilon\leq 1/2
.
Let
H\sim\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{1/2+\varepsilon/2}
and
G\sim\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{\varepsilon/2}
.
Note that
H\cup G\sim\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{\eta}
, for some
\eta\leq 1/2+\varepsilon
.
Furthermore, by
Lemma

5.6
, a.a.s.
\delta(H)\geq\delta n
.
Applying
Theorem

1.7
to
H\cup G
, we obtain the desired result.
∎

9.
Hitting time result

In this section we prove
Theorem

1.8
and
Theorem

1.5
.
The proof of
Theorem

1.8
closely follows that of
Theorem

1.7
, with some necessary changes to deal with the possibility that some vertices have degree
o(n)
.
We will describe here all necessary changes.
It is worth noting that the set
\mathcal{A}
in
Theorem

7.1
is only needed for this section.

Theorem

1.8
will actually follow from a slightly more general result (see
Theorem

9.6
), which we prove in
Section

9.3
.
Similarly as for
Theorem

1.7
, we will first prove
Theorem

1.8
for the property of being Hamiltonian (that is, the case
k=2
), and then use this to prove the general result.

Let
s\in\mathbb{N}
.
Throughout this section, we consider a similar setup to that in
Section

8
.
In particular, we have a partition of the vertex set of the hypercube into layers
L_{1},\ldots,L_{2^{s}}
, where the labelling of the layers is given by some Hamilton cycle of
\mathcal{Q}^{s}
.
We fix any such partition, and will write
\hat{e}_{i}
for the direction of the edges between the layers
L_{i}
and
L_{i+1}
.
We will use the notation for atoms, molecules and slices in the same way.
We will also use part of the notation introduced at the beginning of the proof of
Theorem

8.1
; in particular, we will consider the intersection graph
I\cong\mathcal{Q}^{n-s}
of the layers, and for any
G\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
we define
I(G)
analogously.

9.1.
Absorbing structures for vertices with small degree

The main reason why the proof of
Theorem

8.1
does not work for the case
k=2
of
Theorem

1.8
is the existence of vertices of very low degree (as low as degree
2
).
We cannot hope to absorb these via absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs as in the proof of
Theorem

8.1
, as they may not have any neighbours which lie in the cube factor we construct.
Thus, we first construct alternative absorbing structures for these vertices.

To be more precise, recall that in the proof of
Theorem

8.1
we absorbed vertices in pairs, in order to compensate the parities (within a vertex molecule).
Here, we will need something similar.
To achieve this, we will define several paths.
One path will contain the vertex of low degree, while the others are used to compensate the parities of vertices in this first path.
Moreover, the paths will be constructed in such a way that they end in vertices which can be paired up so that each pair consists of clones of a given vertex of the intersection graph
I
and these two clones lie in a (bonded) cube molecule.
These cube molecules can then be used to connect these paths.
It is worth mentioning, however, that we cannot guarantee that the pairing of the vertices can be done within a single slice, so we need to alter our approach to deal with this.

These special absorbing structures will be used in a somewhat different way to the absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs (which are still used to absorb all other vertices).
For the latter, recall that we enforce a condition on the near-spanning cycle
\mathfrak{H}
(namely, that it contains certain edges
e_{\mathrm{abs}}(u)
) so that we can absorb the required vertices
u\in V^{\mathrm{abs}}
.
For the special absorbing structures, however, we will actually enforce that the vertices of very low degree are already incorporated into
\mathfrak{H}
.
In particular, consider the cube molecules which contain the endpoints of paths of one of these special absorbing structures.
When constructing the skeleton, we will add extra segments in these molecules which will be used to connect these paths in such a way that, when we apply the connecting lemmas, we can completely incorporate each special absorbing structure into
\mathfrak{H}
.

Suppose
x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
is incident to one edge with direction
a
and one edge with direction
b
.
We will construct three different types of special absorbing structures, to handle the cases where both, only one, or none of these two edges lie in the same layer as
x
.
Representations of these three types of special absorbing structures can be found in
Fig.

3
.
Let
L
be the layer such that
x\in V(L)
.
Given a path
P=x_{1}x_{2}\ldots x_{k}
in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, we define
\mathrm{end}(P)\coloneqq\{x_{1},x_{k}\}
.

Type I.

Assume that
x+a,x+b\in V(L)
.
Let
f\colon V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})\to V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
be defined as follows: for each
i\in[2^{s}]
and each
y\in V(L_{i})
, if
i
is even, we set
f(y)\coloneqq y+\hat{e}_{i-1}
; otherwise, we set
f(y)\coloneqq y+\hat{e}_{i}
.
By abusing notation, for any
F\subseteq L_{i}
, we also consider the graph
f(F)
, where for each edge
e=\{y,z\}\in E(F)
we define
f(e)\coloneqq\{f(y),f(z)\}
.
Let
(c,d,d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4})\in(\mathcal{D}(L)\setminus\{a,b\})^{6}
be a tuple of distinct directions and define the following paths in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
:

•

P_{1}\coloneqq(x+a+d_{1},x+a,x,x+b,x+b+d_{2})
;

•

P_{2}\coloneqq(f(x+b+d_{2}),f(x+b),f(x+b+c))
;

•

P_{3}\coloneqq(x+c+b,x+c,x+c+d_{3})
;

•

P_{4}\coloneqq(f(x+c+d_{3}),f(x+c),f(x),f(x+d),f(x+d+d_{4}))
;

•

P_{5}\coloneqq(x+d+d_{4},x+d,x+d+a)
;

•

P_{6}\coloneqq(f(x+a+d),f(x+a),f(x+a+d_{1}))
.

Observe that, for every
y\in V(P_{1}\cup\dots\cup P_{6})
, we have that
f(y)=f^{-1}(y)\in V(P_{1}\cup\dots\cup P_{6})
.
We say that
\mathit{CS}(x,a,b)\coloneqq(P_{1},\ldots,P_{6})
is an
(x,a,b)
-
consistent system of paths
, and let
\mathrm{end}(\mathit{CS}(x,a,b))\coloneqq\bigcup_{i=1}^{6}\mathrm{end}(P_{i})
.

Let
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{6})
be an
(x,a,b)
-consistent system of paths.
Let
\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\{c,d,d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4}\}\subseteq\mathcal{D}(L)
be the set of directions such that
P_{1},\ldots,P_{6}
are as defined above.
Let
\mathbf{C}\coloneqq\{C_{1},\ldots,C_{12}\}
be a collection of vertex-disjoint
\ell
-cubes which satisfy the following:

(\mathrm{PI}.1)

for all
i\in[12]
, we have that either
C_{i}\subseteq L
or
C_{i}\subseteq f(L)
;

(\mathrm{PI}.2)

for all
i\in[6]
, we have
f(C_{2i})=C_{2i+1}
, where indices are taken modulo
12
;

(\mathrm{PI}.3)

for all
i\in[6]
,
C_{2i-1}
contains the first vertex of
P_{i}
, and
C_{2i}
contains the last vertex of
P_{i}
;

(\mathrm{PI}.4)

for all
i\in[12]
, we have
\mathcal{D}(C_{i})\cap(\mathcal{D}\cup\{a,b\})=\varnothing
.

We say that
\mathit{SA}(x,a,b)\coloneqq(P_{1},\ldots,P_{6},C_{1},\ldots,C_{12})
is an
(x,a,b)
-
special absorbing structure
.

Finally, given any graph
G\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
and an
(x,a,b)
-consistent system of paths
\mathit{CS}(x,a,b)=(P_{1},\ldots,P_{6})
, we say that
\mathit{CS}(x,a,b)

extends
to an
(x,a,b)
-special absorbing structure in
G
if there is a collection
\mathbf{C}=\{C_{1},\ldots,C_{12}\}
with
C_{i}\subseteq G
such that
\mathit{SA}(x,a,b)=(P_{1},\ldots,P_{6},C_{1},\ldots,C_{12})
is an
(x,a,b)
-special absorbing structure.

Type II.

Assume now that
x+a,x+b\notin V(L)
.
Let
(d_{1},d_{2})\in(\mathcal{D}(L))^{2}
be a pair of distinct directions and define the following paths in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
:

•

P_{1}\coloneqq(x+a+d_{1},x+a,x,x+b,x+b+d_{2})
;

•

P_{2}\coloneqq(x+a+b+d_{2},x+a+b,x+a+b+d_{1})
.

We say that
\mathit{CS}(x,a,b)\coloneqq(P_{1},P_{2})
is an
(x,a,b)
-
consistent system of paths
, and let
\mathrm{end}(\mathit{CS}(x,a,b))\coloneqq\mathrm{end}(P_{1})\cup\mathrm{end}(P_{2})
.

Let
(P_{1},P_{2})
be an
(x,a,b)
-consistent system of paths.
Let
\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\{d_{1},d_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{D}(L)
be the set of directions such that
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
are as defined above.
Let
L_{ab}
,
L_{a}
and
L_{b}
be the layers such that
x+a+b\in V(L_{ab})
,
x+a\in V(L_{a})
and
x+b\in V(L_{b})
, respectively.
Let
\mathbf{C}\coloneqq\{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}\}
be a collection of vertex-disjoint
\ell
-cubes which satisfy the following:

(\mathrm{PII}.1)

C_{1}\subseteq L_{a}
,
C_{2}\subseteq L_{b}
and
C_{3},C_{4}\subseteq L_{ab}
;

(\mathrm{PII}.2)

C_{1}+b=C_{4}
and
C_{2}+a=C_{3}
;

(\mathrm{PII}.3)

for all
i\in[2]
,
C_{2i-1}
contains the first vertex of
P_{i}
, and
C_{2i}
contains the last vertex of
P_{i}
;

(\mathrm{PII}.4)

for all
i\in[4]
, we have
\mathcal{D}(C_{i})\cap\mathcal{D}=\varnothing
.

We say that
\mathit{SA}(x,a,b)\coloneqq(P_{1},P_{2},C_{1},\ldots,C_{4})
is an
(x,a,b)
-
special absorbing structure
.

Finally, given any graph
G\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
and an
(x,a,b)
-consistent system of paths
\mathit{CS}(x,a,b)=(P_{1},P_{2})
, we say that
\mathit{CS}(x,a,b)

extends
to an
(x,a,b)
-special absorbing structure in
G
if there is a collection
\mathbf{C}=\{C_{1},\ldots,C_{4}\}
with
C_{i}\subseteq G
such that
\mathit{SA}(x,a,b)=(P_{1},P_{2},C_{1},\ldots,C_{4})
is an
(x,a,b)
-special absorbing structure.

Type III.

Finally, assume that
x+a\notin V(L)
and
x+b\in V(L)
.
For each vertex
y\in V(L)
, let
f(y)\coloneqq y+a
.
By abusing notation, for any
F\subseteq L
, we also consider the graph
f(F)
, where for each edge
e=\{y,z\}\in E(F)
we define
f(e)\coloneqq\{f(y),f(z)\}
.
Let
(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3})\in(\mathcal{D}(L)\setminus\{b\})^{3}
be a tuple of distinct directions and define the following paths in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
:

•

P_{1}\coloneqq(f(x+d_{1}+d_{2}),f(x+d_{1}),f(x),x,x+b,x+b+d_{3})
;

•

P_{2}\coloneqq(f(x+b+d_{3}),f(x+b),f(x+b+d_{1}))
;

•

P_{3}\coloneqq(x+d_{1}+b,x+d_{1},x+d_{1}+d_{2})
.

We say that
\mathit{CS}(x,a,b)\coloneqq(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})
is an
(x,a,b)
-
consistent system of paths
, and let
\mathrm{end}(\mathit{CS}(x,a,b))\coloneqq\mathrm{end}(P_{1})\cup\mathrm{end}(P_{2})\cup\mathrm{end}(P_{3})
.

Let
(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})
be an
(x,a,b)
-consistent system of paths.
Let
\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\{d_{1},d_{2},d_{3}\}\subseteq\mathcal{D}(L)
be the set of directions such that
P_{1}
,
P_{2}
and
P_{3}
are as defined above.
Let
\mathbf{C}\coloneqq\{C_{1},\ldots,C_{6}\}
be a set of vertex-disjoint
\ell
-cubes which satisfy the following:

(\mathrm{PIII}.1)

for all
i\in[6]
, we have that
C_{i}\subseteq L
or
C_{i}\subseteq f(L)
;

(\mathrm{PIII}.2)

for all
i\in[3]
, we have
C_{2i}=f(C_{2i+1})
, where indices are taken modulo
6
;

(\mathrm{PIII}.3)

for all
i\in[3]
,
C_{2i-1}
contains the first vertex of
P_{i}
, and
C_{2i}
contains the last vertex of
P_{i}
;

(\mathrm{PIII}.4)

for all
i\in[6]
, we have
\mathcal{D}(C_{i})\cap(\mathcal{D}\cup\{b\})=\varnothing
.

We say that
\mathit{SA}(x,a,b)\coloneqq(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3},C_{1},\ldots,C_{6})
is an
(x,a,b)
-
special absorbing structure
.

Finally, given any graph
G\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
and an
(x,a,b)
-consistent system of paths
\mathit{CS}(x,a,b)=(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})
, we say that
\mathit{CS}(x,a,b)

extends
to an
(x,a,b)
-special absorbing structure in
G
if there is a collection
\mathbf{C}=\{C_{1},\ldots,C_{6}\}
with
C_{i}\subseteq G
such that
\mathit{SA}(x,a,b)=(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3},C_{1},\ldots,C_{6})
is an
(x,a,b)
-special absorbing structure.

(a)

Type I

(b)

Type II

(c)

Type III

Figure 3
.
A representation of the special absorbing structures.
In each case, vertices (or cubes) represented in vertical lines are clones of the same vertex (or cube) of the intersection graph
I
, and any vertices in the same horizontal line lie in the same layer of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
.

Whenever
x
,
a
and
b
are clear from the context, we will simply write
\mathit{CS}
and
\mathit{SA}
instead of
\mathit{CS}(x,a,b)
and
\mathit{SA}(x,a,b)
.
Given any consistent system of paths
\mathit{CS}
, we let
\mathrm{endmol}(\mathit{CS})
be the set of vertices
v\in V(I)
such that some clone of
v
lies in
\mathrm{end}(\mathit{CS})
.
We write
\mathcal{D}(\mathit{CS})
to denote the set of directions
\mathcal{D}\cup\{a,b\}
used to define the paths which comprise
\mathit{CS}
as above.
If
\mathit{CS}
extends to a special absorbing structure
\mathit{SA}
, we denote
\mathrm{end}(\mathit{SA})\coloneqq\mathrm{end}(\mathit{CS})
.
Moreover, we denote by
\mathbf{C}(\mathit{SA})
the collection of cubes associated with
\mathit{SA}
.
Observe that

(

⁢
PI
.4

)

,

(

⁢
PII
.4

)

and

(

⁢
PIII
.4

)

imply that

(AS)

each cube
C\in\mathbf{C}(\mathit{SA})
is vertex-disjoint from the paths in
\mathit{SA}
except for the unique vertex in
\mathrm{end}(\mathit{SA})
contained in
C
.

We will sometimes abuse notation and treat
\mathit{CS}
and
\mathit{SA}
as graphs; in particular, we will write
V(\mathit{CS})
to denote the vertices of the union of the paths which comprise
\mathit{CS}
, and
V(\mathit{SA})
to denote the vertices of the union of the paths and cubes which comprise
\mathit{SA}
, and similarly for
E(\mathit{CS})
and
E(\mathit{SA})
.

9.2.
Auxiliary lemmas

We now state and prove some auxiliary lemmas.
When taking random subgraphs of the hypercube, we will need to guarantee that, given a vertex in
I
and a large collection of cubes in
I
incident to this vertex, some of the cube molecules given by these cubes are bonded.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
\varepsilon,\gamma\in(0,1)
and
\ell,n\in\mathbb{N}
with
0<1/n\ll 1/\ell\ll\varepsilon,\gamma
, and let
s\coloneqq 10\ell
.
Let
x\in V(I)
and let
\mathcal{C}
be a collection of
\ell
-cubes
C\subseteq I
such that
|\mathcal{C}|\geq\gamma n^{\ell}
and, for all
C\in\mathcal{C}
, we have
x\in V(C)
.
For each
C\in\mathcal{C}
, let
\mathcal{M}_{C}
denote the cube molecule of
C
in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
.
For any graph
G\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, let

B(G)\coloneqq\{C\in\mathcal{C}:\mathcal{M}_{C}\text{ is bonded in }G\}.

Then, with probability at least
1-2^{-10n}
, we have
|B(\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{\varepsilon})|\geq\gamma n^{\ell}/4
.

Proof.

Let
G\sim\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{\varepsilon}
and let
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\coloneqq\{C-(N_{I}(x)\cup\{x\}):C\in\mathcal{C}\}
.
Given
C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, for each
i\in[2^{s}]
, let
C^{\prime}_{i}
be the
i
-th clone of
C^{\prime}
.
We denote
\mathcal{M}_{C^{\prime}}\coloneqq C^{\prime}_{1}\cup\dots\cup C^{\prime}_{2^{s}}
, and refer to it as the molecule of
C^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
.
We say that
\mathcal{M}_{C^{\prime}}
is
bonded in
G
if, for each
i\in[2^{s}]
, the graph
G
contains at least
100
edges between
C^{\prime}_{i}
and
C^{\prime}_{i+1}
whose endpoints in
C^{\prime}_{i}
have odd parity and
100
edges whose endpoints in
C^{\prime}_{i}
have even parity, where indices are taken cyclically.
Note that, if
C^{\prime}=C-(N_{I}(x)\cup\{x\})
for some
C\in\mathcal{C}
and
C^{\prime}
is bonded in
G
, then
C
must be bonded in
G
.
Moreover,
|V(C^{\prime})|=|V(C)|-\ell-1>9|V(C)|/10
.
Therefore, similarly to the proof of
Lemma

8.3
, by
Lemma

4.2
and a union bound we have that

\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{M}_{C^{\prime}}\text{ is bondless in }G]\leq 2^{s-\varepsilon 2^{\ell}/100}\leq 1/2.

Let
X\coloneqq|\{C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}:\mathcal{M}_{C^{\prime}}\text{ is bonded in }G\}|
.
It follows that

(9.1)

\mathbb{E}[|B(G)|]\geq\mathbb{E}[X]\geq\gamma n^{\ell}/2.

Let
V\coloneqq\bigcup_{C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}V(\mathcal{M}_{C^{\prime}})
.
Let
e_{1},\dots,e_{m}
be an arbitrary ordering of the edges of
E\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\in[2^{s}]}E_{\mathcal{Q}^{n}}(L_{i}\cap V,L_{i+1}\cap V)
.
For each
j\in[m]
, let
X_{j}
be the indicator variable which takes value
1
if
e_{j}\in E(G)
and
0
otherwise.
Consider the edge-exposure martingale
Y_{j}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[X\mid X_{1},\dots,X_{j}]
for
j\in[m]_{0}
.
This is a Doob martingale with
Y_{0}=\mathbb{E}[X]
and
Y_{m}=X
.

We will now bound the differences
|Y_{j}-Y_{j-1}|
, for all
j\in[m]
.
For each
i\in[\ell]\setminus\{1\}
, let
N^{i}(x)\coloneqq\{y\in\bigcup_{C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}V(C^{\prime}):{\rm dist}(x,y)=i\}
.
Let
E^{i}\subseteq E
be the collection of edges
e=(u,v)
where both
u
and
v
are clones of a vertex
z\in N^{i}(x)
.
Note that the sets
E^{2},\dots,E^{\ell}
partition
E
.
Moreover, for each
j\in[m]
, if
e_{j}\in E^{i}
, then
|Y_{j}-Y_{j-1}|\leq n^{\ell-i}
.
Furthermore,
|E^{i}|=2^{s}|N^{i}(x)|\leq 2^{s}n^{i}
and, thus,

\sum_{j\in[m]}|Y_{j}-Y_{j-1}|^{2}\leq\sum_{i=2}^{\ell}|E^{i}(x)|n^{2\ell-2i}\leq\sum_{i=2}^{\ell}2^{s}n^{i}n^{2\ell-2i}=\mathcal{O}(n^{2\ell-2}).

Therefore, we can apply
Lemma

4.5
and combine it with (
9.1
) to obtain

\mathbb{P}[|B(G)|<\gamma n^{\ell}/4]\leq\mathbb{P}[X<\gamma n^{\ell}/4]\leq\mathbb{P}[|X-\mathbb{E}[X]|\geq\mathbb{E}[X]/2]\leq e^{-10n.}\qed

The following observation will also be used repeatedly.

Remark 9.2
.

Let
n,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
with
1/n\ll\eta^{\prime}<\eta
.
Let
x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be a collection of
\ell
-cubes
C\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
such that
x\in V(C)
for all
C\in\mathcal{C}
and
|\mathcal{C}|\geq\eta n^{\ell}
.
Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
be a set of directions with
|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}|\leq\eta^{\prime}n
.
Then, there exists a cube
C\in\mathcal{C}
with
\mathcal{D}(C)\cap\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\varnothing
.

Proof.

Observe that the number of
\ell
-cubes
C\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
with
x\in C
and
\mathcal{D}(C)\cap\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\neq\varnothing
is at most
\eta^{\prime}n\cdot n^{\ell-1}
.
Since
\eta>\eta^{\prime}
, we are done.
∎

As discussed in
Section

2.6
, a crucial requirement for the proof of
Theorem

1.8
will be that vertices of very low degree in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{1/2-\varepsilon}
are few and far apart.
Moreover, we will also require some more properties about the distribution of these vertices, and that, for all of them, we can find many candidates for special absorbing structures.
We express all this information in the following definition.

Definition 9.3
.

Let
n,s,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
with
1/n\ll 1/s\leq 1/\ell
, and let
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\gamma\in[0,1]
.
Fix an ordering of the layers
L_{1},\ldots,L_{2^{s}}
of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
induced by any Hamilton cycle in
\mathcal{Q}^{s}
(as defined in
Section

8.1
).
Let
G\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
be a spanning subgraph.
For any
\varepsilon>0
, let
\mathcal{U}(G,\varepsilon)\coloneqq\{x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n}):d_{G}(x)<\varepsilon n\}
.
Let
\mathcal{U}\subseteq V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
be a set of size
|\mathcal{U}|\leq 2^{\varepsilon_{2}n}
.
We say that
G
is
(s,\ell,\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\gamma,\mathcal{U})
-robust
if the following properties are satisfied:

(\mathrm{R}1)

\mathcal{U}(G,\varepsilon_{1})\subseteq\mathcal{U}
.

(\mathrm{R}2)

For all
x\in\mathcal{U}
and every
y\in B_{\mathcal{Q}^{n}}^{s+5\ell}(x)\setminus\{x\}
, we have
d_{G}(y)\geq\gamma n
.

(\mathrm{R}3)

For all
x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
, we have
|\mathcal{U}\cap B_{\mathcal{Q}^{n}}^{\gamma n}(x)|\leq 1
.

(\mathrm{R}4)

For all
x\in\mathcal{U}
and any distinct directions
a,b\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
, there exists a collection
\mathfrak{C}(x,a,b)
of
(x,a,b)
-consistent systems of paths in
G\cup\{\{x,x+a\},\{x,x+b\}\}
which satisfies the following.
Let
L
be the layer containing
x
.

(\mathrm{R}4.\mathrm{I})

Suppose
x+a,x+b\in V(L)
.
Then, there exists a collection
\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a,b)
of disjoint pairs of distinct directions
c,d\in\mathcal{D}(L)\setminus\{a,b\}
such that
|\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a,b)|\geq\gamma n
and, for every
(c,d)\in\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a,b)
, there is a collection
\mathcal{D}^{(4)}(x,a,b,c,d)
of disjoint
4
-tuples of distinct directions in
\mathcal{D}(L)\setminus\{a,b,c,d\}
with
|\mathcal{D}^{(4)}(x,a,b,c,d)|\geq\gamma n
satisfying the following property:
for each
(c,d)\in\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a,b)
and each
(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4})\in\mathcal{D}^{(4)}(x,a,b,c,d)
, the
(x,a,b)
-consistent system of paths
\mathit{CS}(c,d,d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4})=(P_{1},\ldots,P_{6})
defined as in
Section

9.1
belongs to
\mathfrak{C}(x,a,b)
.

(\mathrm{R}4.\mathrm{II})

Suppose
x+a,x+b\notin V(L)
.
Then, there exists a collection
\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a,b)
of disjoint pairs of distinct directions
d_{1},d_{2}\in\mathcal{D}(L)
such that
|\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a,b)|\geq\gamma n
and, for every
(d_{1},d_{2})\in\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a,b)
, the
(x,a,b)
-consistent system of paths
\mathit{CS}(d_{1},d_{2})=(P_{1},P_{2})
defined as in
Section

9.1
belongs to
\mathfrak{C}(x,a,b)
.

(\mathrm{R}4.\mathrm{III})

Suppose
x+a\notin V(L)
and
x+b\in V(L)
.
Then, there exists a set
\mathcal{D}(x,a,b)
of directions
d_{1}\in\mathcal{D}(L)
such that
|\mathcal{D}(x,a,b)|\geq\gamma n
and, for every
d_{1}\in\mathcal{D}(x,a,b)
, there exists a collection
\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a,b,d_{1})
of disjoint pairs of distinct directions in
\mathcal{D}(L)\setminus\{b,d_{1}\}
with
|\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a,b,d_{1})|\geq\gamma n
satisfying the following property:
for each
d_{1}\in\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a,b)
and each
(d_{2},d_{3})\in\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a,b,d_{1})
, the
(x,a,b)
-consistent system of paths
\mathit{CS}(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3})=(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})
defined as in
Section

9.1
belongs to
\mathfrak{C}(x,a,b)
.

(\mathrm{R}5)

Let
x_{1}\coloneqq\{0\}^{n}
,
x_{2}\coloneqq\{1\}^{n}
,
x_{3}\coloneqq\{1\}^{\lceil n/2\rceil}\{0\}^{n-\lceil n/2\rceil}
and
x_{4}\coloneqq\{0\}^{\lceil n/2\rceil}\{1\}^{n-\lceil n/2\rceil}
.
Then, for each
i\in[4]
we have
\mathcal{U}\cap B_{\mathcal{Q}^{n}}^{s+\ell}(x_{i})=\varnothing
.

Lemma 9.4
.

Let
1/n\ll 1/s\leq 1/\ell\ll\varepsilon_{1}\ll\varepsilon\ll\varepsilon_{2}\ll\gamma\ll 1/r
with
n,s,\ell,r\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then,

(i)

a.a.s.
G\sim\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{1/2-\varepsilon}
is
(s,\ell,\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\gamma,\mathcal{U}(G,\varepsilon_{1}))
-robust, and

(ii)

given any
\mathcal{U}\subseteq V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
with
|\mathcal{U}|\leq 2^{\varepsilon_{2}n}
and any
H\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
which is
(s,\ell,\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\gamma,\mathcal{U})
-robust, there exists an edge-decomposition
H=H_{1}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}\dots\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}H_{r}
such that for each
i\in[r]
we have that
H_{i}\subseteq H
is spanning and
(s,\ell,\varepsilon_{1}/(2r),\varepsilon_{2},\gamma/r^{10},\mathcal{U})
-robust.

Proof.

We begin with a proof of
(i)
.
Let
\mathcal{U}\coloneqq\mathcal{U}(G,\varepsilon_{1})
.
For any
x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
, we have that

(9.2)

\mathbb{P}[x\in\mathcal{U}]\leq\binom{n}{\varepsilon_{1}n}(1/2+\varepsilon)^{n-\varepsilon_{1}n}<2^{-n+20\varepsilon n}.

It follows that
\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{U}|]<2^{n}2^{-n+20\varepsilon n}=2^{20\varepsilon n}
.
Therefore, by Markov’s inequality we have that

\mathbb{P}[|\mathcal{U}|\geq 2^{\varepsilon_{2}n}]<2^{20\varepsilon n}/2^{\varepsilon_{2}n}<2^{-\varepsilon_{2}n/2},

so a.a.s.
|\mathcal{U}|\leq 2^{\varepsilon_{2}n}
.

(

⁢
R
1

)

holds trivially by the choice of
\mathcal{U}
.
Furthermore, we have that

\Bigg|\bigcup_{i\in[4]}B^{s+\ell}_{\mathcal{Q}^{n}}(x_{i})\Bigg|\leq 5n^{s+\ell},

so, by (
9.2
) and a union bound,

(

⁢
R
5

)

also holds a.a.s.

To see that

(

⁢
R
2

)

holds, fix
x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
and
y\in B^{s+5\ell}_{\mathcal{Q}^{n}}(x)
.
Then,
\mathbb{E}[d_{G-\{x\}}(y)]=(1/2-\varepsilon)n\pm 1
.
Thus, by
Lemma

4.2
,

\mathbb{P}[d_{G-\{x\}}(y)\leq\gamma n-1]\leq 2^{-n/20}.

Therefore, by (
9.2
), we have that
\mathbb{P}[x\in\mathcal{U}\wedge d_{G-\{x\}}(y)\leq\gamma n-1]\leq 2^{-n+20\varepsilon n-n/20}\leq 2^{-31n/30}
.
A union bound over all
x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
and over all
y\in B^{s+5\ell}_{\mathcal{Q}^{n}}(x)
shows that

(

⁢
R
2

)

holds a.a.s.

The fact that

(

⁢
R
3

)

holds a.a.s. can be shown similarly.

Finally, consider

(

⁢
R
4

)

.
Let
x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
, and suppose
x\in V(L)
, for some layer
L
.
First, let
a,b\in\mathcal{D}(L)
be distinct.
We are going to show that a.a.s. we can find the desired collection of
(x,a,b)
-consistent systems of paths.

Recall that an
(x,a,b)
-consistent system of paths
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{6})
, as defined in
Section

9.1
, is determined uniquely by a
6
-tuple of directions
(c,d,d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4})
.
In order to show that

(

⁢
R
4

)

(
R
4
.
I
)

is satisfied, we will first consider the directions
c
and
d
, and then the rest of the tuple.
Recall that all
(x,a,b)
-consistent systems of paths contain the two edges
\{x,x+a\}
and
\{x,x+b\}
.
Then, once
c
and
d
are fixed, this determines a total of
6
more edges.
The remaining
8
edges will be determined by the choice of
(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4})
.

Consider a collection
\mathcal{W}
of disjoint pairs of distinct directions
(c,d)
with
c,d\in\mathcal{D}(L)\setminus\{a,b\}
such that
|\mathcal{W}|\geq n/4
.
For each
(c,d)\in\mathcal{W}
, let
E^{*}(c,d)\subseteq E(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
be the set of six edges of an
(x,a,b)
-consistent system of paths determined by these two directions.
Observe that, since the pairs in
\mathcal{W}
are disjoint, it follows that, for any distinct
(c,d),(c^{\prime},d^{\prime})\in\mathcal{W}
, we have
E^{*}(c,d)\cap E^{*}(c^{\prime},d^{\prime})=\varnothing
.
Now let
\mathcal{W}_{G}\coloneqq\{(c,d)\in\mathcal{W}:E^{*}(c,d)\subseteq E(G)\}
and
X\coloneqq|\mathcal{W}_{G}|
.
We have that
\mathbb{E}[X]\geq(1/2-\varepsilon)^{6}n/4
and, by
Lemma

4.2
, it follows that
\mathbb{P}[X\leq\gamma n]\leq 2^{-\gamma n}
.

For each
(c,d)\in\mathcal{W}
, let
\mathcal{V}(c,d)
be a collection of disjoint
4
-tuples of distinct directions
(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4})
with
d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4}\in\mathcal{D}(L)\setminus\{a,b,c,d\}
such that
|\mathcal{V}(c,d)|\geq n/5
.
For each
(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4})\in\mathcal{V}(c,d)
, let
E^{*}(c,d,d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4})\subseteq E(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
be the set of eight edges of an
(x,a,b)
-consistent system of paths determined by
(c,d,d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4})
which are not in
E^{*}(c,d)\cup\{\{x,x+a\},\{x,x+b\}\}
.
In particular, since the tuples in
\mathcal{V}(c,d)
are disjoint, it follows that, for any distinct
(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4}),(d_{1}^{\prime},d_{2}^{\prime},d_{3}^{\prime},d_{4}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}(c,d)

.
Now let

, and let
Y(c,d)\coloneqq|\mathcal{V}_{G}(c,d)|
.
We then have that
\mathbb{E}[Y(c,d)]\geq(1/2-\varepsilon)^{8}n/5

4.2
, it follows that
\mathbb{P}[Y(c,d)\leq\gamma n]\leq 2^{-\gamma n}
.
Thus, by a union bound, with probability at least

, for every

we have

.

Let

I
)

.

The same bound can be proved for the cases where
a\notin\mathcal{D}(L),b\in\mathcal{D}(L)
and
a,b\notin\mathcal{D}(L)
.
Observe that, for any

and

)

holds a.a.s.

The proof of

is similar.

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
0<1/n\ll 1/C\ll\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\ll\gamma,\delta\leq 1
and
1/n\ll 1/s\leq 1/\ell
, where

.
Let

and

be such that

is
(s,\ell,\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\gamma,\mathcal{U})
-robust.
Let
\mathcal{U}_{I}\subseteq V(I)
be the set of vertices
u\in V(I)
such that
\mathcal{U}
contains some clone of
u
.
For each
v\in V(I)
and each

, let
v_{i}
be the
i
-th clone of
v
, and let

.
Let

and, for each

be the
i
-th clone of

.
Let

Let

be the event that there exists some

such that
|B_{I}^{10\ell}(u)\cap B|\geq C
.
Let

be the event that there exists some
u\in\mathcal{U}_{I}
such that
|B_{I}^{5\ell}(u)\cap B|\geq 1
.
Then,
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{1}\vee\mathcal{E}_{2}]\leq 1/n
.

Proof.

Let
u\in V(I)
and let

be a set of
C
vertices.
Let
D^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigcup_{x,y\in D:x\neq y}N_{I}(x)\cap N_{I}(y)
.
Since any pair of distinct vertices in

share at most two neighbours, we have that
|D^{\prime}|\leq 2\binom{C}{2}
.
For each
i\in[2^{s}]
, we denote the
i
-th clone of

by

, and let

.

For each

, let
i(x)
be the unique index
i\in[2^{s}]
such that
x\in V(L_{i})
.
Observe that, by

(

⁢
R
1

)

, we have
e_{H}(x,V(L_{i(x)}))>2\varepsilon_{1}n/3
for every
x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})\setminus\mathcal{U}
.
For each
x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
, let
\mathcal{E}_{x}
be the event that
e_{H}(x,R_{i(x)})\leq\varepsilon_{1}\delta n/4
, and let
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{x}
be the event that

.
It follows by
Lemma

4.2
that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{x}]\leq e^{-\varepsilon_{1}\delta n/16}

for all
x\in V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})\setminus\mathcal{U}
.
For each
v\in V(I)
, let
\mathcal{E}_{v}
and
\mathcal{E}_{v}^{\prime}
be the events that there exists
i\in[2^{s}]
with
v_{i}\notin\mathcal{U}
such that
\mathcal{E}_{v_{i}}
and

hold, respectively.
By a union bound, it follows that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{v}^{\prime}]\leq 2^{s}e^{-\varepsilon_{1}\delta n/16}
for all

Finally, let
\mathcal{E}_{D}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{D}
be the events that

and
\mathcal{E}_{v}^{\prime}

.
Note that the events in the collection

are mutually independent.
Furthermore, since the event

for all

By a union bound over all

and over all choices of

.

Consider now any

.
Observe that, if

, then for every

.
Therefore, by

(

⁢
R
2

)

and

with

9.3.
Hamilton cycles in robust subgraphs of the cube

It will be useful to prove the following result, which (together with
Lemma

9.4
) directly implies the case
k=2
of
Theorem

1.8
(by choosing
H
in
Theorem

1.8
to play the role of
H^{\prime}
in
Theorem

9.6
and
F
to be empty).
As with
Theorem

8.1
, the formulation of
Theorem

9.6
is designed so that the case
k>2
can be derived easily (see
Section

9.4
).
To state the result, we need the following notation.

Given any integers
s\leq n
, we say that
d\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
is an
s
-direction
if its only non-zero coordinate is one of the first
s
coordinates.
Given a graph
F\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, a set
\mathcal{U}\subseteq V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
and
\ell,s\in\mathbb{N}
, we say that
F
is
(\mathcal{U},\ell,s)
-good
if, for each
x\in\mathcal{U}
, the set
E_{F}(x)\coloneqq\{e\in E(F):e\cap N_{\mathcal{Q}^{n}}(x)\neq\varnothing\}
satisfies that, for each
d\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
which is not an
s
-direction, we have
|\{e\in E_{F}(x):\mathcal{D}(e)=d\}|\leq n/\ell
.
Thus, a graph is good if locally the directions of its edges are not too correlated (ignoring
s
-directions).
The goodness of the ‘forbidden’ graph
F
below will be needed when finding the special absorbing structures (see Step 11).

Theorem 9.6
.

Let
0<1/\ell\ll\varepsilon_{1}\ll\varepsilon_{2}\ll\gamma\leq 1
and
1/\ell\ll\eta,1/c\leq 1
, with
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
s\coloneqq 10\ell
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then, there exists
\Phi\in\mathbb{N}
such that the following holds.

Let
H\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
and
\mathcal{U}\subseteq V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
be such that
H
is an
(s,\ell,\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\gamma,\mathcal{U})
-robust subgraph, and let
Q\sim\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{\eta}
.
Then, a.a.s. there is a
(\mathcal{U},\ell^{2},s)
-good subgraph
Q^{\prime}\subseteq Q
with
\Delta(Q^{\prime})\leq\Phi
such that

•

for every
H^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, where
d_{H^{\prime}}(x)\geq 2
for every
x\in\mathcal{U}
, and

•

for every
F\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
with
\Delta(F)\leq c\Phi
which is
(\mathcal{U},\ell,s)
-good,

we have that
((H\cup Q)\setminus F)\cup H^{\prime}\cup Q^{\prime}
contains a
(\mathcal{U},\ell^{2},s)
-good Hamilton cycle
C
such that, for all
x\in\mathcal{U}
, both edges of

incident to

belong to
H^{\prime}
.

As we have already discussed, the proof of

Thus, we will avoid repeating all the details which are analogous, and we will often refer back the proof of

A full sequential proof can be found in

.

We now split our proof into several steps.

Step 1. Finding a tree and a reservoir.

.

Note that, by

(

⁢
R
3

)

.

R

For each

Condition on

Then, there exist a set

)

–

)

Step 3: Finding clustered robust matchings for each molecule.

As in the proof of
Theorem

8.1
, the aim is to find auxiliary matchings which can later be used to pair up vertices which need to be absorbed.
In the proof of
Theorem

8.1
, we were able to carry out this pairing within each slice.
However, we cannot guarantee that the vertices of each special absorbing structure of Type II and III will lie within a single slice.
There will be an even number of these vertices (zero, two or four) within each vertex molecule, but there might be exactly one within a slice of this molecule, making it impossible to pair up vertices within a slice.
Thus we now consider the entire vertex molecule when finding the auxiliary matching (rather than each slice separately).
This would normally make it much more difficult to link up vertices of the skeleton in Step 18.
We are able to overcome this problem by considering matchings which are ‘clustered’, i.e. the endpoints of each matching edge either lie in a common slice or in two consecutive slices.

Let
q\coloneqq 2^{10Dk^{*}}
and
t\coloneqq 2^{s}/q
, where
k^{*}
satisfies (
9.4
).
For each
j\in[t]
, let
S_{j}\coloneqq\bigcup_{i=(j-1)q+1}^{jq}L_{i}
.
Given any molecule
\mathcal{M}
, we define the slices
\mathcal{S}_{j}(\mathcal{M})\coloneqq S_{j}\cap\mathcal{M}
.
We denote by
\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{M})
the collection of all these slices of
\mathcal{M}
.

Let
V_{\mathrm{sc}}\subseteq V(I)
be the set of all vertices
x\in V(I)
such that
\mathcal{M}_{x}
is scant.
In particular, by

(

⁢
T
4

)

we have that
V_{\mathrm{sc}}\cap\mathcal{U}_{I}=\varnothing
.
Recall
G_{2}\sim\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{\eta_{2}}
.
For each
v\in V(I)\setminus(V_{\mathrm{sc}}\cup\mathcal{U}_{I})
, we define auxiliary bipartite graphs
H(v)\coloneqq(V(\mathcal{M}_{v}),N_{I}(v),E_{H})
and
G_{2}(v)\coloneqq(V(\mathcal{M}_{v}),N_{I}(v),E_{G_{2}})
, where
E_{H}
and
E_{G_{2}}
are defined as in Step 3 of the proof of
Theorem

8.1
for vertices
v\in V(I)\setminus V_{\mathrm{sc}}
(but now the first vertex class of
H(v)
and
G_{2}(v)
is
V(\mathcal{M}_{v})
rather than
V(\mathcal{S})
for some slice
\mathcal{S}
).
For each
v\in V_{\mathrm{sc}}
, we similarly define
H(v)
and
G_{2}(v)
as we did in Step 3 of the proof of
Theorem

8.1
, with the same modifications as above.

For each
v\in\mathcal{U}_{I}
, we also define two such auxiliary graphs.
Let
H(v)\coloneqq(V(\mathcal{M}_{v}),N_{I}(v),E_{H}^{*})
, where
E_{H}^{*}
is defined as follows.
Consider
v^{\prime}\in V(\mathcal{M}_{v})
and let
L^{v^{\prime}}
be the layer which contains
v^{\prime}
.
Let
w\in N_{I}(v)
, and let
w_{L^{v^{\prime}}}
be the clone of
w
in
L^{v^{\prime}}
.
Then, if
v^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}
, we add
\{v^{\prime},w\}
to
E_{H}^{*}
(these can be seen as purely auxiliary edges, and we will ignore their effect later).
Otherwise,
\{v^{\prime},w\}\in E_{H}^{*}
if and only if
w\in R
and
\{v^{\prime},w_{L^{v^{\prime}}}\}\in E(H)
.
In particular,
d_{H(v)}(v^{\prime})\geq\varepsilon_{1}\delta^{\prime}n/10
for all
v^{\prime}\in V(\mathcal{M}_{v})
since
\mathcal{M}_{v}
is a not a scant molecule.
We define
G_{2}(v)\coloneqq(V(\mathcal{M}_{v}),N_{I}(v),E_{G_{2}})
, where
\{v^{\prime},w\}\in E_{G_{2}}
if and only if
\{v^{\prime},w_{L^{v^{\prime}}}\}\in E(G_{2})
.

For every
v\in V(I)
and every slice
\mathcal{S}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{M}_{v})
, note that the partition of
V(\mathcal{S})
into vertices of even and odd parity is a balanced bipartition.
Define the graph
\Gamma^{\beta}_{H(v),G_{2}(v)}(V(\mathcal{M}_{v}))
as in
Section

5.1
, where
\beta
satisfies (
9.4
).
Thus,
V(\Gamma^{\beta}_{H(v),G_{2}(v)}(V(\mathcal{M}_{v})))=V(\mathcal{M}_{v})
.
Furthermore, by definition,

(\mathrm{RM})

given any
w_{1},w_{2}\in V(\mathcal{M}_{v})
, we have that
\{w_{1},w_{2}\}\in E(\Gamma^{\beta}_{H(v),G_{2}(v)}(V(\mathcal{M}_{v})))
if and only if
|N_{H(v)}(w_{1})\cap N_{G_{2}(v)}(w_{2})|\geq\beta(n-s)
or

|N_{G_{2}(v)}(w_{1})\cap N_{H(v)}(w_{2})|\geq\beta(n-s)
.

For each
i\in[t]
, let
\mathfrak{A}_{i}(v)
consist of all vertices of
V(\mathcal{S}_{i}(\mathcal{M}_{v}))
of even parity, and let
\mathfrak{B}_{i}(v)
consist of those of odd parity.
By applying
Corollary

5.3
with
d=100D
,
\alpha=\varepsilon_{1}\delta^{\prime}/10
,
\varepsilon=\eta_{2}
,
n=n-s
,
k=q=2^{10Dk^{*}}
,
\beta=\beta
,
t=t
,
G=H(\mathcal{M}_{v})
and
V(\mathcal{S}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{v}))\cup\ldots\cup V(\mathcal{S}_{t}(\mathcal{M}_{v}))
as a partition of
V(\mathcal{M}_{v})
, we obtain that, with probability at least
1-2^{-9(n-s)}\geq 1-2^{-8n}
, the graph
\Gamma^{\beta}_{H(v),G_{2}(v)}(V(\mathcal{M}_{v}))
is
100D
-robust-parity-matchable with respect to
(\bigcup_{i=1}^{t}\mathfrak{A}_{i}(v),\bigcup_{i=1}^{t}\mathfrak{B}_{i}(v))
clustered in
(V(\mathcal{S}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{v})),\ldots,V(\mathcal{S}_{t}(\mathcal{M}_{v})))
.

By a union bound over all
v\in V(I)
, a.a.s.
\Gamma^{\beta}_{H(v),G_{2}(v)}(V(\mathcal{M}_{v}))
is
100D
-robust-parity-matchable with respect to
(\bigcup_{i=1}^{t}\mathfrak{A}_{i}(v),\bigcup_{i=1}^{t}\mathfrak{B}_{i}(v))
clustered in
(V(\mathcal{S}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{v})),\ldots,V(\mathcal{S}_{t}(\mathcal{M}_{v})))
for every
v\in V(I)
.
We condition on this event holding and call it
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{*}
.
Thus, for each
v\in V(I)
and each set
X\subseteq V(\mathcal{M}_{v})
with
|X|\leq 100D
which contains as many odd vertices as even vertices, there exists a perfect matching
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{M}_{v},X)
in the bipartite graph with parts consisting of the even and odd vertices of
V(\mathcal{M}_{v})\setminus X
, respectively, and edges given by
\Gamma^{\beta}_{H(v),G_{2}(v)}(V(\mathcal{M}_{v}))
, with the property that, for each
e=\{w_{\mathrm{e}},w_{\mathrm{o}}\}\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{M}_{v},X)
, if
w_{\mathrm{e}}\in V(\mathcal{S}_{i}(\mathcal{M}_{v}))
for some
i\in[t]
, then
w_{\mathrm{o}}\in V(\mathcal{S}_{i-1}(\mathcal{M}_{v}))\cup V(\mathcal{S}_{i}(\mathcal{M}_{v}))\cup V(\mathcal{S}_{i+1}(\mathcal{M}_{v}))
(where indices are taken cyclically).
When we apply this in Step 15, we will have
\mathcal{U}\cap V(\mathcal{M}_{v})\subseteq X
.

For each
v\in V(I)\setminus\mathcal{U}_{I}
, we denote by
\mathfrak{M}(v)
the set of edges contained in the union (over all
X
) of the matchings
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{M}_{v},X)
(without multiplicity).
For each
v\in\mathcal{U}_{I}
, we let
u(v)
be the unique vertex in
\mathcal{M}_{v}
such that
u(v)\in\mathcal{U}
, and we denote by
\mathfrak{M}(v)
the set of edges contained in the union of the matchings
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{M}_{v},X)
over all
X
such that
u(v)\in X
(again, without multiplicity).
Furthermore, for each
v\in V(I)
and each
e=\{w_{\mathrm{e}},w_{\mathrm{o}}\}\in\mathfrak{M}(v)
, we let
N(e)\coloneqq(N_{H(v)}(w_{\mathrm{e}})\cap N_{G_{2}(v)}(w_{\mathrm{o}}))\cup(N_{G_{2}(v)}(w_{\mathrm{e}})\cap N_{H(v)}(w_{\mathrm{o}}))
.
By

(
RM
)

, we have
|N(e)|\geq\beta(n-s)\geq\beta n/2
.
Let
K\coloneqq\max_{v\in V(I)}|\mathfrak{M}(v)|
.
Thus,
K\leq\binom{2^{s}}{2}
.

Step 4: Obtaining an appropriate cube factor via the nibble.

For each
x\in V(I)

Step 5: Absorption cubes.

Recall
G_{4}\sim\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{\eta_{4}}
.
We define the event

.
Upon conditioning on this event, for each

and

Consider
A_{i}(x)
, for some

and

(\mathrm{AB}1)

-cube pair

-cube pair for

in

In particular, the edge joining the left absorber tip to the third absorber vertex lies in

.
Moreover,

and

and

We refer to

-cube pairs for

and

associated

Recall by the construction in Step 3 that, for each

and

, we have
x_{1},x_{2}\notin\mathcal{U}
.
In particular, this means that we do not choose absorbing

-cube pairs for the vertices in

and, thus, the auxiliary edges at the vertices in

introduced in the definition of the sets

in Step 3 will never be used.
As discussed before, the vertices in
\mathcal{U}
will instead be incorporated into the Hamilton cycle using the special absorbing structures introduced in
Section

.

Step 8. Consistent systems of paths and cubes.

Recall
G_{7}\sim\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{\eta_{7}}
.
For each
v\in V(I)
, let
\mathcal{C}(v)\coloneqq\{C\subseteq I(G_{7}):C\cong\mathcal{Q}^{\ell},v\in V(C)\}
.
Let
\mathcal{P}\coloneqq\{v\in V(I):|\mathcal{C}(v)|\geq\lambda n^{\ell}\}
, where
\lambda
satisfies (
9.4
).
By
Remark

5.13
(applied with

and
\eta_{7}^{2^{s}}
playing the role of
\varepsilon
), the following property holds a.a.s.

(\mathrm{D}1)

For every
v\in V(I)
we have
|B_{I}^{10}(v)\setminus\mathcal{P}|\leq n^{7/8}
.

For each
v\in\mathcal{P}
, a straightforward application of
Lemma

9.1
with
\lambda
and
\mathcal{C}(v)
playing the roles of
\gamma
and
\mathcal{C}
, respectively, shows that the following holds with probability at least

:
there exists a subcollection
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(v)\subseteq\mathcal{C}(v)
, with
|\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(v)|\geq\lambda n^{\ell}/4
, with the property that, for every
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(v)
, the molecule
\mathcal{M}_{C}
is bonded in
G_{7}
.
By a simple union bound over all vertices in
\mathcal{P}
, we obtain that the following holds a.a.s.

(\mathrm{D}2)

For every
v\in\mathcal{P}
, there exists a collection
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(v)\subseteq\mathcal{C}(v)
with
|\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(v)|\geq\lambda n^{\ell}/4
such that, for every
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(v)
, we have that
\mathcal{M}_{C}
is bonded in
G_{7}
.

Condition on the event that

(

⁢
D
1

)

and

(

⁢
D
2

)

hold and call it
\mathcal{E}_{7}^{*}
.

We will show that we may extend many of the consistent systems of paths given by

(

⁢
R
4

)

into special absorbing structures.
Recall that, since
H
is
(s,\ell,\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\gamma,\mathcal{U})
-robust, for every
x\in\mathcal{U}
and each pair of directions
a,b\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
, there exists a collection
\mathfrak{C}(x,a,b)
of
(x,a,b)
-consistent systems of paths in

(

⁢
R
4

)

.
By

(

⁢
D
1

)

, for every
x\in\mathcal{U}
and
a,b\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{n})

(CS)

there exists a subcollection
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}(x,a,b)\subseteq\mathfrak{C}(x,a,b)
which satisfies

(

⁢
R
4

)

with
\gamma/2
playing the role of
\gamma
and such that, for every
\mathit{CS}\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}(x,a,b)
, we have
\mathrm{endmol}(\mathit{CS})\subseteq\mathcal{P}
.

Let
L
be any layer of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
.
For each
x\in\mathcal{U}
, let
\mathrm{End}(x)\coloneqq\bigcup_{a,b\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{n})}\bigcup_{\mathit{CS}\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}(x,a,b)}\mathrm{endmol}(\mathit{CS})
, let
\mathrm{End\,II}(x)\coloneqq\bigcup_{a,b\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{n})\setminus\mathcal{D}(L)}\bigcup_{\mathit{CS}\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}(x,a,b)}\mathrm{endmol}(\mathit{CS})
, and let
x_{I}\in V(I)
be such that
x
is a clone of
x_{I}
.
Given any tree

and any cube

, we say that

if

.
Recall that, for any
y,z\in V(I)
, we denote the set of directions in a shortest path in
I
between

and

by
\mathcal{D}(y,z)
.

Claim 9.1
.

For each
x\in\mathcal{U}
and each
z\in\mathrm{End}(x)
, there exists a collection of cubes
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z)\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(z)
with
|\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z)|=20\ell
which satisfies the following properties.

(\mathrm{i})

Every
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z)
meets
T^{\prime}
.

(\mathrm{ii})

For every
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z)
, we have
\mathcal{D}(C)\cap\mathcal{D}(x_{I},z)=\varnothing
.

(\mathrm{iii})

For every
C_{1},C_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z)
, we have
V(C_{1})\cap V(C_{2})=\{z\}
.

(\mathrm{iv})

For each
x\in\mathcal{U}
, let
\bm{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}(x)\coloneqq\bigcup_{z\in\mathrm{End\,II}(x)}\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z)
.
For each
d\in\mathcal{D}(L)
and each
x\in\mathcal{U}
, let
\alpha(d,x)\coloneqq|\{C\in\bm{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}(x):d\in\mathcal{D}(C)\}|
.
For all
d\in\mathcal{D}(L)
and
x\in\mathcal{U}
, we have that
\alpha(d,x)\leq n/\ell^{3}
.

Proof.

Fix any vertex
x\in\mathcal{U}
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}\coloneqq|\mathrm{End}(x)|
and
m\coloneqq|\mathrm{End\,II}(x)|
, and consider an arbitrary labelling
z_{1},\ldots,z_{m},z_{m+1},\ldots,z_{\mathfrak{m}}
of the vertices in
\mathrm{End}(x)
such that all vertices in
\mathrm{End\,II}(x)
come first.
Observe that, by the definition of Type II consistent systems of paths (see
Section

9.1
), we have that
m\leq n
.
We will now iteratively define the sets
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z_{i})
for each
i\in[\mathfrak{m}]
.

Fix first any
i\in[m]
, and suppose that a set
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z_{j})
satisfying the claim is already defined for all
j\in[i-1]
.
Let
\bm{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}(x,i)\coloneqq\bigcup_{j=1}^{i-1}\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z_{j})
.
For each
d\in\mathcal{D}(L)
, let
\alpha(d,x,i)\coloneqq|\{C\in\bm{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}(x,i):d\in\mathcal{D}(C)\}|
.
Let
\mathcal{D}^{*}\coloneqq\{d\in\mathcal{D}(L):\alpha(d,x,i)\geq n/\ell^{3}\}
.
Observe that
|\mathcal{D}^{*}|\leq 20\ell^{5}
.
Let
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{i})
be the set of all cubes
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(z_{i})
such that
C
meets
T^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{D}(C)\cap(\mathcal{D}(x_{I},z_{i})\cup\mathcal{D}^{*})=\varnothing
(i.e., they satisfy

(
i
)

and

(
ii
)

and, if added to the collection, would not violate

(
iv
)

).
We claim that
|\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{i})|\geq\lambda n^{\ell}/5
.
Indeed, observe that
{\rm dist}(x_{I},z_{i})\leq 2
and, thus, the number of cubes
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(z_{i})
such that
\mathcal{D}(C)\cap(\mathcal{D}(x_{I},z_{i})\cup\mathcal{D}^{*})\neq\varnothing
is at most
(20\ell^{5}+2)n^{\ell-1}
.
(To see this, note that, since
{\rm dist}(x_{I},z_{i})\leq 2
, we have that
|\mathcal{D}(x_{I},z_{i})|\leq 2
.
Now, for each direction
\hat{e}\in\mathcal{D}(x_{I},z_{i})\cup\mathcal{D}^{*}
, the number of
\ell
-cubes in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
containing
z
and some edge in direction
\hat{e}
is
\binom{n}{\ell-1}<n^{\ell-1}
.)
Moreover, by

(

⁢
ET
2

)

and

(

⁢
R
3

)

, the number of such cubes which do not meet
T^{\prime}
is at most
n^{\ell-2}
.
The bound then follows by

(

⁢
D
2

)

.

We can now construct
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z_{i})
by obtaining cubes
C_{1}(z_{i}),\ldots,C_{20\ell}(z_{i})
iteratively.
Note that, for any pair of cubes
C_{1},C_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(z_{i})
, we have that
z\in V(C_{1})\cap V(C_{2})
.
Then,

(
iii
)

is equivalent to having that
\mathcal{D}(C_{1})\cap\mathcal{D}(C_{2})=\varnothing
.
For each
k\in[20\ell]
, we proceed as follows.
Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{k}\coloneqq\bigcup_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathcal{D}(C_{j}(z_{i}))
.
Note that
|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{k}|\leq 20\ell^{2}\leq\lambda n/8
.
Now, applying
Remark

9.2
with
n-s
,
z
,
\lambda/5
,
\lambda/8
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{k}
playing the roles of
n
,
x
,
\eta
,
\eta^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, respectively, we deduce that there is a cube
C_{k}(z_{i})\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{i})
with
\mathcal{D}(C_{k}(z_{i}))\cap\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{k}=\varnothing
.
By enforcing that

(
iii
)

holds, it follows that each direction is used at most once in the cubes that were added in this step.
It then follows that

(
iv
)

holds as well.

Consider now any
i\in[\mathfrak{m}]\setminus[m]
, and suppose that a set
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z_{j})
satisfying the claim is already defined for all
j\in[i-1]
.
Let
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{i})
be the set of all cubes
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(z_{i})
such that
C
meets
T^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{D}(C)\cap\mathcal{D}(x_{I},z_{i})=\varnothing
(i.e., they satisfy

(
i
)

and

(
ii
)

).
As above, we claim that
|\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{i})|\geq\lambda n^{\ell}/5
.
Indeed, the number of cubes
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(z_{i})
such that
\mathcal{D}(C)\cap\mathcal{D}(x_{I},z_{i})\neq\varnothing
is at most
2n^{\ell-1}
, and, again, the number of such cubes which do not meet
T^{\prime}
is at most
n^{\ell-2}
.
The bound then follows by

(

⁢
D
2

)

.

We can now construct
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z_{i})
as above.
For each
k\in[20\ell]
, we proceed as follows.
Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{k}\coloneqq\bigcup_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathcal{D}(C_{j}(z_{i}))
.
Note that
|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{k}|\leq 20\ell^{2}\leq\lambda n/8
.
Now, applying
Remark

9.2
with
n-s
,
z
,
\lambda/5
,
\lambda/8
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{k}
playing the roles of
n
,
x
,
\eta
,
\eta^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, respectively, we deduce that there is a cube
C_{k}(z_{i})\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{i})
with
\mathcal{D}(C_{k}(z_{i}))\cap\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{k}=\varnothing
.
∎

Let
J^{1}\coloneqq\bigcup_{x\in\mathcal{U}}\bigcup_{z\in\mathrm{End}(x)}\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z)}\mathcal{M}_{C}
, where
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z)
are the sets given by
9.1
, and let
G^{*}_{7}\subseteq G_{7}
consist of all edges of
G_{7}
which have endpoints in different layers.

Claim 9.2
.

J^{1}\cup G^{*}_{7}
is
(\mathcal{U},\ell^{3},s)
-good and
\Delta(J^{1}\cup G^{*}_{7})\leq 50\ell^{4}
.

Proof.

In order to see that
J^{1}\cup G^{*}_{7}
is
(\mathcal{U},\ell^{3},s)
-good, observe first that the edges of
G^{*}_{7}
do not affect this definition, so it suffices to see that
J^{1}
is
(\mathcal{U},\ell^{3},s)
-good.
By
9.1

(
ii
)

, for all
x\in\mathcal{U}
,
z\in\mathrm{End}(x)
and
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z)
we have that
{\rm dist}(x_{I},C)={\rm dist}(x_{I},z)
.
In particular, by the definition of the different consistent systems of paths (see
Section

9.1
), it follows that the only cubes which affect whether
J^{1}
is
(\mathcal{U},\ell^{3},s)
-good or not are those of the collection
\bm{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}(x)
described in
9.1

(
iv
)

.
But then, by
9.1

(
iv
)

, we have that no direction
d\in\mathcal{D}(L)
is used more than
n/\ell^{3}
times, as required.

Note that
\Delta(G_{7}^{*})\leq s=10\ell
, by construction.
We will now show that
\Delta(J^{1})\leq 45\ell^{4}
.
Observe that
J^{1}
does not contain any edges with endpoints in different layers.
In particular,
J^{1}
consists of clones of the same subgraph of
I
, that is
I(J^{1})=\bigcup_{x\in\mathcal{U}}\bigcup_{z\in\mathrm{End}(x)}\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z)}C
.
By this observation, it is enough to show that
\Delta(I(J^{1}))\leq 45\ell^{4}
.

Recall that, by

(

⁢
R
3

)

, given any distinct
x,y\in\mathcal{U}_{I}
, we have that
{\rm dist}(x,y)\geq\gamma n/2
.
In particular, by this observation and
9.1

(
ii
)

, it follows that, for all
x\in\mathcal{U}_{I}
, we have
d_{I(J^{1})}(x)=0
.
We also note that, for every
z\in V(I)
for which
{\rm dist}(z,\mathcal{U}_{I})\geq\ell+3
, we have
d_{I(J^{1})}(z)=0
.
Now, fix any
x\in\mathcal{U}_{I}
and
z\in V(I)
such that
{\rm dist}(z,x)=t
, for some
t\in[\ell+2]
.
We claim that
d_{I(J^{1})}(z)\leq 2t^{2}\cdot 20\ell^{2}\leq 45\ell^{4}
.

Suppose first that
t=1
.
Then, by
9.1

(
ii
)

, for every edge
e\in E(I(J^{1}))
incident with
z
, we have
e\in E(C)
for some
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z)
, and hence
d_{I(J^{1})}(z)\leq\ell|\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z)|=20\ell^{2}
, as we wanted to show.
Suppose now that
t\geq 2
and let
\mathcal{D}(z,x)=\{d_{1},\ldots,d_{t}\}
.
Every edge
e
incident with
z
must come from the edges of a cube
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(w)
, for some
w\in\mathrm{End}(x)
.
Moreover, by
9.1

(
ii
)

, we must have
\mathcal{D}(x,w)\subseteq\mathcal{D}(x,z)
.
As there are at most
t+t^{2}\leq 2t^{2}
vertices
w\in V(I)
such that
\mathcal{D}(x,w)\subseteq\mathcal{D}(x,z)
and
{\rm dist}(x,w)\in[2]
, we have have that
d_{I(J^{1})}(z)\leq 2t^{2}20\ell^{2}
, which concludes the proof of the claim.
∎

Step 9: Fixing a collection of absorbing
\bm{\ell}
-cube pairs for the vertices in scant molecules.

In this step, we use
G_{8}\sim\mathcal{Q}^{n}_{\eta_{8}}
to alter

so that any tips of absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs for vertices
x\in V_{\mathrm{sc}}
which do not lie in
R
are relocated from the tree
T^{\prime}
to the reservoir.
We follow the same approach as in Step 8 of the proof of
Theorem

8.1
.
In particular, we define an event
\mathcal{E}_{8}^{*}
(which is analogous to
\mathcal{E}_{7}^{*}
in the proof of
Theorem

8.1
) and condition that it holds.
This then gives a set of absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sc}}_{1}=\{(C^{l}(x,j,k),C^{r}(x,j,k))\subseteq I:x\in V_{\mathrm{sc}},j\in[K],k\in[2^{s+1}\Psi]\}
, where each
(C^{l}(x,j,k),C^{r}(x,j,k))
is an absorbing
\ell
-cube pair for
x
, which satisfies that

(\mathrm{CD})

for all distinct
(x,j,k),(x^{\prime},j^{\prime},k^{\prime})\in V_{\mathrm{sc}}\times[K]\times[2^{s+1}\Psi]
,
C^{l}(x,j,k)
and
C^{r}(x,j,k)
are both vertex-disjoint from
C^{l}(x^{\prime},j^{\prime},k^{\prime})
and
C^{r}(x^{\prime},j^{\prime},k^{\prime})
.

We define

and
P
as in Step 8 of the proof of
Theorem

8.1
.
Observe that

(

⁢
T
4

)

implies that
(P^{\prime}\cup V(P))\cap B^{2\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I})=\varnothing
.
Let
T^{\textsc{iv}}\coloneqq T^{\prime}[V(T^{\prime})\setminus P^{\prime}]\cup P
, which is connected, and let
T^{\prime\prime}
be a spanning tree of
T^{\textsc{iv}}
.
In particular, it follows from the above and the definitions of
T
and
T^{\prime}
in Steps 1 and 7 that

(9.7)

T^{\prime\prime}\subseteq I(G_{1}\cup G_{6}\cup G_{8})-B_{I}^{5}(\mathcal{U}_{I})\subseteq I-B_{I}^{5}(\mathcal{U}_{I}).

Furthermore, as in Step 8 of the proof of
Theorem

8.1
, we have that

(9.8)

\Delta(T^{\prime\prime})\leq 12D.

Define the (new) reservoir
R^{\prime}\coloneqq(R\cup P^{\prime})\setminus V(P)
.

For each
x\in V(I)\setminus B^{5}_{I}(\mathcal{U}_{I})
, let

(where

is as defined in Step 7).
It follows by (

) and

(

⁢
T
3

)

that

|Z^{\prime}(x)|\geq 3n/4-4\cdot 2^{s}\Psi KS^{\prime}\geq n/2.

Choose any vertex
x\in V(I)
with
{\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{U}_{I})\geq 3\ell
.
Again by

(

⁢
T
3

)

, there are at most
4\cdot 2^{s+\ell}\Psi KS^{\prime}
vertices in
Z^{\prime}(x)
which lie in cubes of absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs of
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sc}}_{1}
.
Choose any vertex
y\in Z^{\prime}(x)
which does not lie in any of those cubes.
Denote the cube
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
which contains

by
C^{\bullet}
.

For each
x\in V(I)\setminus V_{\mathrm{sc}}
and each
i\in[K]
, we now redefine the set

as follows.

(\mathrm{AB}4)

Let

retain only those edges whose associated absorbing
\ell
-cube pair

satisfies that both

and

\ell
-cube pairs of
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sc}}_{1}
, and both tips

and

.

Note that, by

(

⁢
T
3

)

, we have

and

.

Step 10: Fixing a collection of absorbing
\bm{\ell}
-cube pairs for vertices in non-scant molecules and vertices near
\bm{\mathcal{U}}
.

At this point, it is not yet clear which vertices will need to eventually be absorbed into the long cycle we construct.
For vertices in
I
which are ‘far’ from
\mathcal{U}_{I}
, we can already determine those which will have clones that will need to be absorbed (though we cannot yet determine the precise clones).
However, for vertices which are ‘near’
\mathcal{U}_{I}
, we still cannot say which of them will have clones that need to be absorbed (this depends on the special absorbing structure which is fixed once the edges of
H^{\prime}
are revealed).
As a result, we proceed as if all of the clones of vertices in
I
near
\mathcal{U}_{I}
will need to be absorbed.
Recall that
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
were introduced in Step 6.
Let

\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}\coloneqq\{C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}:V(C)\cap V(T^{\prime\prime})\neq\varnothing\}\quad\text{ and }\quad V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}\coloneqq(V(I)\setminus\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}}V(C))\cup B_{I}^{3\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I}).

We will now fix a collection of absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs for all vertices in each vertex molecule
\mathcal{M}_{x}
with
x\in V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}\setminus V_{\mathrm{sc}}
, except for the vertices of
\mathcal{U}
.

Similarly as in (
8.13
), we have

(9.10)

|B^{2\ell}_{I}(x)\setminus(V(T^{\prime\prime})\cup B_{I}^{5}(\mathcal{U}_{I}))|\leq 2n^{3/4}.

For all
x\in\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}V(C)
, by combining

(

⁢
N
1

)

, (
9.10
) and

(

⁢
R
3

)

with the definition of bondlessly surrounded molecules, we claim that

(9.11)

|\mathcal{C}_{x}\cap\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}|\geq(1-2^{-\ell-5s+1})n,

where
\mathcal{C}_{x}
is the collection of all those
C\in\mathcal{C}
such that
x\notin V(C)
and
N_{I}(x)\cap V(C)\neq\varnothing
.
Indeed, combining

(

⁢
N
1

)

with the definition of bondlessly surrounded, it follows that
|\mathcal{C}_{x}\cap\mathcal{C}^{\prime}|\geq(1-\delta)n-n/2^{\ell+5s}
. Now, since
|B_{I}^{2\ell}(x)\cap\mathcal{U}_{I}|\leq 1
by

(

⁢
R
3

)

, every cube in
\mathcal{C}_{x}
has at least half of its vertices outside
B_{I}^{5}(\mathcal{U}_{I})
.
For any cube in
\mathcal{C}_{x}\cap\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
to not belong in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}
, these at least
2^{\ell-1}
vertices which do not lie in

must also avoid
V(T^{\prime\prime})
.
Therefore, by (
9.10
), at most
2n^{3/4}
of these cubes in
\mathcal{C}_{x}\cap\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
to not lie in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
Thus, in total, we have
|\mathcal{C}_{x}\cap\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}|\geq(1-\delta)n-n/2^{\ell+5s}-2n^{3/4}\geq(1-2\cdot 2^{-\ell-5s})n
.

Recall that, for any
x\in V(I)
, each index
i\in[K]
is given by a unique edge
e\in\mathfrak{M}(x)
via the relation
N(e)=A_{i}(x)
.
Recall also the definition of
\mathfrak{M}(x)
from Step 3.
We now prove the following claim, which is similar to
8.2
(apart from the new property

(
ii
)

).

Claim 9.3
.

For each
x\in V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}\setminus V_{\mathrm{sc}}
and each
e\in\mathfrak{M}(x)
, there exists a set
\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\mathrm{abs}}(e)
of
2^{s+1}\Psi
absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs
(C_{k}^{l}(e),C_{k}^{r}(e))\subseteq I
, one for each
k\in[2^{s+1}\Psi]
, which satisfies the following:

(\mathrm{i})

for all
x\in V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}\setminus V_{\mathrm{sc}}
,
e\in\mathfrak{M}(x)
and
k\in[2^{s+1}\Psi]
, the absorbing
\ell
-cube pair
(C_{k}^{l}(e),C_{k}^{r}(e))
is associated with some edge in
M(A_{j}(x))
, for some
j\in[K]
;

(\mathrm{ii})

for all
x\in B_{I}^{3\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I})
,
e\in\mathfrak{M}(x)
and
k\in[2^{s+1}\Psi]
, the absorbing
\ell
-cube pair
(C_{k}^{l}(e),C_{k}^{r}(e))
has tips
x_{k}^{l}(e)
and
x_{k}^{r}(e)
which satisfy that
{\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{U}_{I})<{\rm dist}(x_{k}^{l}(e),\mathcal{U}_{I})
and
{\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{U}_{I})<{\rm dist}(x_{k}^{r}(e),\mathcal{U}_{I})
, and

(\mathrm{iii})

for all
x,x^{\prime}\in V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}\setminus V_{\mathrm{sc}}
, all
e\in\mathfrak{M}(x)
and
e^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{M}(x^{\prime})
, and all
k,k^{\prime}\in[2^{s+1}\Psi]
with
(x,e,k)\neq(x^{\prime},e^{\prime},k^{\prime})
, the absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs
(C_{k}^{l}(e),C_{k}^{r}(e))
and
(C_{k^{\prime}}^{l}(e^{\prime}),C_{k^{\prime}}^{r}(e^{\prime}))
satisfy that
(V(C_{k}^{l}(e))\cup V(C_{k}^{r}(e)))\cap(V(C_{k^{\prime}}^{l}(e^{\prime}))\cup V(C_{k^{\prime}}^{r}(e^{\prime})))=\varnothing
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{V}\coloneqq\bigcup_{x\in V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}\setminus V_{\mathrm{sc}}}\mathfrak{M}(x)
.
Let
K^{\prime}\coloneqq|\mathcal{V}|
, and let
f_{1},\dots,f_{K^{\prime}}
be an ordering of the edges in
\mathcal{V}
.
Given any
i\in[K^{\prime}]
, the edge
f_{i}
corresponds to a pair
(x,j(i))
, where
x\in V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}\setminus V_{\mathrm{sc}}
and
j(i)\in[K]
.
If
x\notin B_{I}^{3\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I})
, let
\mathfrak{C}_{i}
be the collection of absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs for
x
in
I
associated with some edge of
M(A_{j(i)}(x))
.
Otherwise, let
\mathfrak{C}_{i}
be the same collection, after removing all those absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs for which

(
ii
)

does not hold.
Since each
x\in B_{I}^{3\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I})
has at most
3\ell
neighbours
y\in N_{I}(x)
such that
{\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{U}_{I})\geq{\rm dist}(y,\mathcal{U}_{I})
, it follows by (
9.9
) that
|\mathfrak{C}_{i}|\geq n/\ell^{3}
for all
i\in[K^{\prime}]
.
In particular, by

(

⁢
AB
2

)

, each of the absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs
(C^{l},C^{r})
in any of the collections
\mathfrak{C}_{i}
satisfies that
C^{l},C^{r}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be the
2^{s+1}\Psi K^{\prime}
-edge-coloured auxiliary multigraph with
V(\mathcal{H})\coloneqq\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
, which contains one edge of colour
(i,k)\in[K^{\prime}]\times[2^{s+1}\Psi]
between
C
and
C^{\prime}
whenever
(C,C^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{C}_{i}
or
(C^{\prime},C)\in\mathfrak{C}_{i}
.
In particular,
\mathcal{H}
contains at least
n/\ell^{3}
edges of each colour.
We now bound
\Delta(\mathcal{H})
.
Consider any
C\in V(\mathcal{H})
.
Note that, for each edge
e
of
\mathcal{H}
incident to
C
, there exists some
x=x(e)\in V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}\setminus V_{\mathrm{sc}}
such that
C
together with some other cube
C^{\prime}\in V(\mathcal{H})
forms an absorbing
\ell
-cube pair for
x
.
In particular,
x
must be adjacent to
C
in
I
.
Let
\overline{\partial}(C)\coloneqq\{x(e):e\in E(\mathcal{H})\text{ is incident to }C\}
.
Moreover, if
e
has colour
(i,z)
, then
f_{i}\in\mathfrak{M}(x)
(and
f_{i}
has corresponding pair
(x,j(i))
for some
j(i)\in[K]
).
Since
f_{i}\in\mathfrak{M}(x)
and
|\mathfrak{M}(x)|\leq\binom{2^{s}}{2}
, it follows that each vertex
y
which is adjacent to
C
in
I
can play the role of
x
for at most
2^{s+1}\Psi\cdot 2^{2s}
edges of
\mathcal{H}
incident to
C
.
Thus,
d_{\mathcal{H}}(C)\leq 2^{3s+1}\Psi|\overline{\partial}(C)|
.

Fix a cube
C\in V(\mathcal{H})
.
In order to bound
|\overline{\partial}(C)|
, consider first
|\overline{\partial}(C)\cap B_{I}^{3\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I})|
.
Recall that, by

(

⁢
R
3

)

, there is at most one vertex
z\in\mathcal{U}_{I}\cap B_{I}^{3\ell}(V(C))
.
Furthermore, since the property described in

(
ii
)

holds for all absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs for vertices in
B_{I}^{3\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I})
represented in
\mathcal{H}
, it follows that each vertex
x\in V(C)
has at most
3\ell+1
neighbours in
B_{I}^{3\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I})\cap\overline{\partial}(C)
.
Thus, in total,
|\overline{\partial}(C)\cap B_{I}^{3\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I})|\leq(3\ell+1)2^{\ell}
.
Consider now
|\overline{\partial}(C)\setminus B_{I}^{3\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I})|
.
Note that
\overline{\partial}(C)\setminus B_{I}^{3\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I})\subseteq(V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}\cap N_{I}(V(C)))\setminus B_{I}^{3\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I})\subseteq N_{I}(V(C))\setminus\bigcup_{C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}}V(C^{\prime})
.
By (
9.11
), the number of vertices in
V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}\setminus B_{I}^{3\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I})
which are adjacent to
C
is at most
2|C|n/{2^{\ell+5s}}
, that is,
|\overline{\partial}(C)\setminus B_{I}^{3\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{I})|\leq 2n/2^{5s}
.
We conclude that
|\overline{\partial}(C)|\leq 3n/2^{5s}
and, thus,
d_{\mathcal{H}}(C)\leq 2^{3s+1}\Psi 3n/{2^{5s}}\leq n/\ell^{4}
.

Since each colour class has size at least
n/\ell^{3}
and
\Delta(\mathcal{H})\leq n/\ell^{4}
, by
Lemma

5.5
,
\mathcal{H}
contains a rainbow matching of size
2^{s+1}\Psi K^{\prime}
.
For each
(i,z)\in[K^{\prime}]\times[2^{s+1}\Psi]
, let
(C_{z}^{l}(f_{i}),C_{z}^{r}(f_{i}))\in\mathfrak{C}_{i}
be the absorbing
\ell
-cube pair of colour
(i,z)
in this rainbow matching.
This ensures that

(
iii
)

holds, while

(
i
)

and

(
ii
)

follow by construction.
∎

For each
x\in V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}\setminus V_{\mathrm{sc}}
and each
i\in[K]
, let
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{abs}}_{1}(x,i)\coloneqq\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{abs}}_{1}(e)
be the set of absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs guaranteed by
9.3
, where
e\in\mathfrak{M}(x)
is the unique edge such that
A_{i}(x)=N(e)
.
Similarly, for each
k\in[2^{s+1}\Psi]
, let
(C^{l}(x,i,k),C^{r}(x,i,k))\coloneqq(C_{k}^{l}(e),C_{k}^{r}(e))
.
Let
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{abs}}_{1}\coloneqq\bigcup_{x\in V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}\setminus V_{\mathrm{sc}}}\bigcup_{i\in[K]}\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{abs}}_{1}(x,i)
.

Let
G\coloneqq\bigcup_{i=1}^{8}G_{i}
.
Recall that
G^{*}_{7}
and
J^{1}
were defined in Step 8.
We let
Q^{\prime}\subseteq G
be the spanning subgraph with edge set

E(Q^{\prime})\coloneqq E(J^{1})\cup E(G_{4}^{*})\cup E(G_{5}^{*})\cup E(G_{7}^{*})\cup\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}E(\mathcal{M}_{C})\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{2^{s}}E(T^{\prime\prime}_{L_{i}}),

where
G_{4}^{*}
and
G_{5}^{*}
are as defined in Step 9 of the proof of
Theorem

8.1
(but with
V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}
playing the role of
V_{\mathrm{abs}}
).
Note that
\Delta(G_{4}^{*})=1
,
\Delta(G_{5}^{*})\leq s=10\ell
and, since the molecules
\mathcal{M}_{C}
with
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
are vertex disjoint, each vertex in
\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}E(\mathcal{M}_{C})
has degree at most
\ell
.
Combining these bounds with
9.2
and (
9.8
), we have that

Step 11: Fixing special absorbing structures.

From this point onward, every step will be deterministic.
Let
F\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
be any graph with
\Delta(F)\leq\Psi
which is
(\mathcal{U},\ell,s)
-good, that is, for each
x\in\mathcal{U}
, the set
E_{F}(x)\coloneqq\{e\in E(F):e\cap N_{\mathcal{Q}^{n}}(x)\neq\varnothing\}
satisfies the following:

(
F^{enumi}
)

for each layer
L
of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
and all
d\in\mathcal{D}(L)
, we have
|\{e\in E_{F}(x):\mathcal{D}(e)=d\}|\leq n/\ell
.

Let
H^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{n}
be any graph such that, for every
x\in\mathcal{U}
, we have
d_{H^{\prime}}(x)\geq 2
.
For each
x\in\mathcal{U}
, let
\{x,x+a(x)\},\{x,x+b(x)\}\in E(H^{\prime})
, where
a(x),b(x)\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
.
Our goal is to find a
(\mathcal{U},\ell^{2},s)
-good Hamilton cycle in
((H\cup G)\setminus F)\cup H^{\prime}\cup Q^{\prime}
which, for each
x\in\mathcal{U}
, contains the edges
\{x,x+a(x)\}
and
\{x,x+b(x)\}
.
Recall that
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}(x,a(x),b(x))
was defined in Step 8.

Claim 9.5
.

For every
x\in\mathcal{U}
, there exists an
(x,a(x),b(x))
-consistent system of paths
\mathit{CS}(x)\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}(x,a(x),b(x))
such that
(E(\mathit{CS}(x))\setminus\{\{x,x+a(x)\},\{x,x+b(x)\}\})\cap E(F)=\varnothing
.

Proof.

Suppose
x\in V(L)
, for some layer
L
.
Suppose first that
x+a(x),x+b(x)\in V(L)
.
Thus, we must show the existence of an
(x,a(x),b(x))
-consistent system of paths of Type I in
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}(x,a(x),b(x))
with the desired property.
Recall all the notation for consistent systems of paths introduced in
Section

9.1
, as well as
Definition

9.3
.
By
(CS)
, there is a collection
\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a(x),b(x))
of at least
\gamma n/2
disjoint pairs of distinct directions
c,d\in\mathcal{D}(L)\setminus\{a(x),b(x)\}
such that, for each
(c,d)\in\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a(x),b(x))
, there is a collection
\mathcal{D}^{(4)}(x,a(x),b(x),c,d)
of at least
\gamma n/2
disjoint
4
-tuples of distinct directions in
\mathcal{D}(L)\setminus\{a(x),b(x),c,d\}
satisfying the following: for each
(c,d)\in\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a(x),b(x))
and each
(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4})\in\mathcal{D}^{(4)}(x,a(x),b(x),c,d)
,
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}(x,a(x),b(x))
contains the
(x,a(x),b(x))
-consistent system of paths
\mathit{CS}(c,d,d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4})
defined as in
Section

9.1
.
We will now show that there are many such consistent systems of paths which avoid
F
.

The choice of
(c,d)\in\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a(x),b(x))
determines six edges of the consistent system of paths:
e_{1}\coloneqq\{f(x+a(x)),f(x+a(x)+d)\}
,
e_{2}\coloneqq\{f(x+b(x)),f(x+b(x)+c)\}
,
e_{3}\coloneqq\{f(x+c),f(x)\}
,
e_{4}\coloneqq\{f(x),f(x+d)\}
,
e_{5}\coloneqq\{x+c,x+c+b(x)\}
and
e_{6}\coloneqq\{x+d,x+d+a(x)\}
.
Since
f(x)
,
f(x+a(x))
and
f(x+b(x))
are fixed and
\Delta(F)\leq\Psi
, for each
i\in[4]
there are at most
\Psi
choices of
(c,d)\in\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a(x),b(x))
such that
e_{i}\in E(F)
.
Furthermore, by
(

F
enumi

)
, for each
i\in\{5,6\}
there are at most
n/\ell
choices
(c,d)\in\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a(x),b(x))
such that
e_{i}\in E(F)
.
Thus, there exist at least
\gamma n/2-4\Psi-2n/\ell\geq\gamma n/4
choices
(c,d)\in\mathcal{D}^{(2)}(x,a(x),b(x))
such that
e_{i}\in E(H)\setminus E(F)
for all
i\in[6]
.
For any such choice of
(c,d)
, the choice of
(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4})\in\mathcal{D}^{(4)}(x,a(x),b(x),c,d)
now determines the remaining eight edges of an
(x,a(x),b(x))
-consistent system of paths, each with a unique endpoint in
\{x+a(x),x+b(x),f(x+b(x)),x+c,f(x+c),f(x+d),x+d,f(x+a(x))\}
.
It now follows by the fact that
\Delta(F)\leq\Psi
that there are at most
8\Psi
choices of
(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4})\in\mathcal{D}^{(4)}(x,a(x),b(x),c,d)
such that some of these eight edges lies in
E(F)
.
In particular, we may fix a consistent system of paths
\mathit{CS}(x)\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}(x,a(x),b(x))
which satisfies the statement of the claim.

The cases where
x+a(x)\notin L
,
x+b(x)\in L
and where
x+a(x),x+b(x)\notin L
can be shown similarly.
∎

Note that
\mathit{CS}(x)\subseteq(H\setminus F)\cup H^{\prime}
for each
x\in\mathcal{U}
.

Claim 9.6
.

For every
x\in\mathcal{U}
, we can extend
\mathit{CS}(x)
into an
(x,a(x),b(x))
-special absorbing structure
\mathit{SA}(x)
such that the following hold:

(\mathrm{SA}_{\mathrm{i}})

for every
C\in\mathbf{C}(\mathit{SA}(x))
, we have that
\mathcal{M}_{C}\subseteq Q^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{M}_{C}
is bonded in
Q^{\prime}
, and

(\mathrm{SA}_{\mathrm{ii}})

every
C\in\mathbf{C}(\mathit{SA}(x))
meets
T^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

For each
x\in\mathcal{U}
, we iterate through each
z\in\mathrm{endmol}(\mathit{CS}(x))
fixing a cube
C(z)\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(z)
.
This will then determine
\mathbf{C}(\mathit{SA}(x))
, by taking the appropriate clones of
C(z)
.
To see that this can be done, note that
|\mathrm{endmol}(\mathit{CS}(x))|\leq 6
.
For each
z\in\mathrm{endmol}(\mathit{CS}(x))
, by

(

⁢
C
7

m_{i}
as in Step 12 of the proof of

.

Step 15: Fixing absorbing
\bm{\ell}
-cube pairs for the vertices that need to be absorbed.

We can now determine every vertex in
V(\mathcal{Q}^{n})
that will have to be absorbed via absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs into the almost spanning cycle we are going to construct.
Recall from Step 11 that
\mathit{SA}^{v}=\bigcup_{y\in\mathcal{U}}V(\mathit{SA}(y))
.
For every vertex
x\in V(I)
not represented in
\tau_{0}^{\prime}
, we will have to absorb all vertices in
\mathcal{M}_{x}\setminus\mathit{SA}^{v}
.
Furthermore, for each
v\in V(\tau_{0})
which is an inner tree vertex, we will also need to absorb all vertices in
\mathcal{M}_{v}\setminus L^{\bullet}=\mathcal{M}_{v}\setminus(L^{\bullet}\cup\mathit{SA}^{v})
.
(The fact that
\mathcal{M}_{v}\cap\mathit{SA}^{v}=\varnothing
follows by

(

⁢

C
′

3

)

.)
Recall the definition of
V_{\mathrm{abs}}^{\prime}
from Step 10.
Let
V_{\mathrm{abs}}\subseteq V(I)
be the set of all vertices which are not represented in
\tau_{0}^{\prime}
by an atomic vertex.
Therefore,
V_{\mathrm{abs}}
is the set of all vertices
x\in V(I)
such that some clone of
x
needs to be absorbed.
Moreover,
V_{\mathrm{abs}}=V(I)\setminus\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{3}}V(C)
and, thus,

(

⁢

C
′

9

)

.
It follows from (
9.14
) that

(9.15)

|V_{\mathrm{abs}}|\leq 2^{n-s+1}/\ell^{4}.

Now, for each
x\in V_{\mathrm{abs}}
, we will pair all those vertices in
\mathcal{M}_{x}
which need to be absorbed (each pair consisting of one vertex of each parity) and fix an absorbing
\ell
-cube pair for each such pair of vertices.
Recall that a difference between this pairing and the pairing in Step 13 of the proof of
Theorem

8.1
is that, in
Theorem

8.1
, we could guarantee that each pair was contained in one of the slices defined in Step 3.
Since a special absorbing structure might not lie in a single slice, we now cannot guarantee this anymore.
Instead, we can impose that each pair either lies in a slice or in two consecutive slices (with respect to their labelling).
The absorbing
\ell
-cube pair that we fix for each pair will be the one given by

(

⁢

C
′

5

)

or

(

⁢

′

6

)

, depending on whether
x\in V_{\mathrm{sc}}
or not.

For each
x\in V_{\mathrm{abs}}
, let
S(x)\coloneqq V(\mathcal{M}_{x})\cap(L^{\bullet}\cup\mathit{SA}^{v})=V(\mathcal{M}_{x})\cap(L^{\bullet}\cup V(\mathcal{M}_{\mathit{CS}}))
.
It follows by

(

⁢
ES
1

)

–

(

⁢

)

,

(

⁢

and the definition of our special absorbing structures that

and

(Here we also use that
p_{i}\leq 12D-1
for every inner tree vertex
v_{i}
by (
9.12
) and (
9.13
).)
Therefore, the matching
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{M}_{x},S(x))
defined in Step 3 is well defined, and we can use this matching to define our pairing of the vertices in
V(\mathcal{M}_{x})\setminus S(x)
.
Recall that each edge
e\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{M}_{x},S(x))
gives rise to a unique index
i\in[K]
via the relation
N(e)=A_{i}(x)
.
(Here we ignore all those indices

arising by artificially increasing the size of

, see of Step 4 as well as Step 4 in the proof of

.)
For each

, let

be the set of indices

which correspond to edges in

For each
x\in V_{\mathrm{abs}}
and

, as stated in

(

⁢

′

and

\ell
-cube pair for the clones of

corresponding to
(x,i)
.
Let

(Thus, in particular,
V^{\mathrm{abs}}\cap\mathcal{U}=\varnothing
.)
As discussed above, this is the set of all vertices that need to be absorbed via absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs.
Recall that

of absorbing
\ell
-cube pairs such that

(

in the proof of

Each of these pairs lies in a different atom of the same or consecutive slices of

.

(\mathrm{C}^{enumi}3)

(\mathrm{C}^{enumi}4)
